## Definition of done and frozen scope

Finish when G1–G7 below pass on an identified candidate with linked evidence and owner sign-off. Retain all 67 capabilities; a prepared or deployed matrix label is not candidate acceptance. Do not add capabilities during closure. New findings must attach to a gate; scope changes require an explicit recorded decision.

PostgreSQL/PostGIS remains canonical; Gateway and Python services own native policy. Directus, ClickHouse, Redis, Prefect, MQTT, gRPC and ingress remain optional or replaceable adapters. Preserve lifecycle `draft → submitted → verified → published`, independent human review, consent, private evidence, bounded retries, leases, idempotency and additive checksummed migrations. Do not retire a supported caller before replacement parity.

Standard is the primary acceptance profile. Preserve minimal/core/Edge compatibility and AMD64/ARM64 software support. Physical Pi, production scale/failover, public deployment and legacy retirement are separate milestones, never implied by candidate sign-off.

### Future deployment profile design target — deferred

Wasabi supplied the original profile progression on 8 November 2026. Treat
profiles as cumulative compositions of the same Kokonut application and
service contracts: deployments add only the infrastructure their workload
needs, while PostgreSQL/PostGIS remains the operational source of truth. A
capability should not require a platform redesign to move between tiers. Per
Wasabi's latest direction, profile alignment is postponed until after the
current G1–G7 refactor gates; the table is retained as planning context.

| Profile | Original design target | Current source and alignment work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| minimal | PostgreSQL/PostGIS + Kokonut Intelligence for laptops, Raspberry Pi, farm edge computers, and low-cost VPS hosts | The Kernel candidate has PostgreSQL plus one combined app service. Keep this as the compact base; physical-device performance and power/storage behavior remain separate acceptance. |
| edge | Minimal + Mosquitto for sensor-equipped farms | The current `edge` profile adds encrypted local media and resource limits; MQTT/Mosquitto is selected separately with `--mqtt`. Reconcile the named profile composition while preserving explicit certificate, stable client identity, and sensor-ingestion prerequisites. |
| standard | PostgreSQL + separate Kokonut API and worker + Caddy | The Standard overlay provides API/worker separation and Caddy by default. Coolify can replace public Caddy ingress with its Traefik network overlay. |
| analytics | Standard + bounded DuckDB/Parquet analytical jobs and workers; optional Metabase | Current `analytics` selects Minimal + ClickHouse; `analytics --separate-workers` composes Standard + ClickHouse. Parquet export support and a standalone optional Metabase overlay exist, but no DuckDB runtime dependency/profile was found. The original design places bounded DuckDB/Parquet jobs in Analytics and ClickHouse projections in Scale; reconcile the current overlay placement against that separation. |
| scale | Standard + Redis + ClickHouse + multiple workers + gRPC, only where workload requires them | `scale` is intentionally rejected. ClickHouse and gRPC overlays exist, but ClickHouse currently sits under Analytics; no Kernel Redis overlay or supported multi-worker release contract exists yet. Prepare the composition and acceptance contract without claiming the runtime is supported. |

The profile alignment task is to make each tier an additive deployment choice,
not a second implementation of domain behavior. Optional infrastructure must
remain optional for the smaller tiers, and analytics projections, Redis/cache
state, and gRPC transport must not replace canonical PostgreSQL state or
governance rules.

## Current baseline and immediate actions

**8 November G2 source-map continuation:** the consolidated caller table below now explicitly includes Directus authorization/schedule hooks, shared-runtime event maintenance, and mobile private-media cleanup. Their live activation and external callers remain unverified; the Standard VPS intentionally excludes Directus, and all database grants remain unchanged.

### Location setup workflow and candidate acceptance

Wasabi entered Kokonut Adelphi through the Control Plane on 9 November 2026,
exercising the operator setup workflow before provider acceptance. The
authenticated form accepts representative latitude/longitude values and a
WGS84 GeoJSON Polygon by upload or paste, or a boundary as manually entered
longitude/latitude pairs. Migration 752 saves the location, farm, tenant-owner
decision, append-only setup evidence, and limited location-read and draft-only
field-activity grants atomically. It never verifies or publishes field records.
Same-slug setup rows can be completed while preserving baseline and historical
records; cross-tenant ownership conflicts fail closed. The deployed entry point
is `https://hub.kokonut.network/ops/administration`.

### Hub relational form support — candidate deployed; signed-in acceptance open

The current `chore/finalize-kokonut-intelligence-refactor` work adds a second
location-setup path for adding a farm beneath an existing, active, verified
tenant location. It lists only locations with an active owned farm, preserves
the parent location, and requires the new farm polygon to be covered by that
location boundary. Farm-activity draft entry now offers bounded, readable
choices for farm, plot, crop cycle, and farm task; the database trigger checks
that the selected relations remain location- and parent-consistent. Hub
location selectors use bounded names and region/country labels instead of
displaying UUIDs. Migration 755 contains the setup and relation-integrity
changes. Exact-head CI [#43061679933](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/43061679933)
and Security [#43061679859](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/43061679859)
passed on code revision `cbc758f5`. Coolify deployment
`qdickqqlxcj0csivwjplxj1i` and Cloudflare Worker version
`77564893-2c14-4053-9910-0da656fb7bed` serve that revision, and migration 755
is applied. Anonymous route checks passed: app pages and Gateway health
returned 200, while session and relation-choice APIs returned 401. The
pre-migration candidate checkpoint was encrypted, uploaded to `kokonut-backups`,
and verified by readback hashes; a restore rehearsal remains open. Signed-in
browser acceptance of the existing-location farm picker and the farm, plot,
crop-cycle, and farm-task choices remains open. Do not create a test farm or
activity just to inspect these forms. See the linked candidate rollout
evidence for the recorded sequence and service health.

Live OIDC callback testing rejected a synthetic callback with a mismatched
state (login 302, callback 401, resulting session 401); evidence is in
[`g4-candidate-oidc-state-mismatch.json`](builds/2026-11-11-recovery/g4-candidate-oidc-state-mismatch.json).
Negative ID-token nonce validation, the second-location denial, and Field
Collector offline/media/consent/sync acceptance remain open. Provider schedules
remain disabled; existing one-shot and credential evidence does not establish
populated Adelphi parity or recurring candidate operation.

The first authenticated organization-list request exposed a transaction mode
issue: a read-only transaction conflicted with the session resolver's
`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`. The bounded listing path now permits the resolver's
session lock while keeping its business query read-only. On the subsequent Save,
the capability check passed, but the setup function's membership ordering read
`organization_member.created_at`, a column omitted from the function owner's
column grants. PostgreSQL raised `42501`, which the Gateway incorrectly mapped
to “account is not authorized.” Migration 753 grants that single required column
to `kokonut_location_setup_owner`; the Gateway now maps only the explicit
missing-capability error to 403 and reports other database permission failures
as 503. No caller identity or capability grant was broadened.

Commit `d5c32d06c446824c609a2c7e27db97bc124f6c89` passed exact-head CI
[#42648564386](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/42648564386)
and Security/Gitleaks [#42648565021](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/42648565021),
was deployed as Coolify deployment `jln5t7uq8bspnnkkgkhwagl2`, and migration
753 is applied with no pending migrations. Live smoke checks return HTTP 200
from `/health` and HTTP 401 from the unauthenticated setup API. Wasabi confirmed
successful setup on 9 November 2026: location
`1b1775d2-3043-42f9-9967-5d3f18db7ae0` and farm
`827aaa01-e8a5-47f4-87d1-e43dbda9e425` were created under Kokonut Network. The
UI confirmed that existing baseline and historical records were preserved and
the farm is ready for data entry. No provider request or reference-data import
was run. Successful setup links to the existing operator console for supported
location-scoped field activity; other farm data-entry screens remain in
migration. G4/G5 are not closed by source, deployment, HTTP checks, or this
initial farm setup alone.

The TypeScript `/field/records` page includes a farm-activity draft form when the authenticated session has the exact location-scoped `farm_activity:create_draft` and `farm_activity:read_detail` grants. It calls the existing Gateway draft endpoint, validates the returned location and draft identity/status, and keeps the selected location fixed while the form has unsaved values. The form cannot submit, verify, publish, attach media, or replace Field Collector offline sync. It does not yet collect plot, crop-cycle, or farm-task references. Frontend typecheck/build and exact-head CI/Security passed on `b0958ef7` ([CI #42732349484](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/42732349484); [Security #42732349756](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/42732349756)). The page displayed one recent record as `Draft — unverified` from `kokonut_gateway`. The unrelated-location read returned 403 with no data. Draft creation passed. At that rollout checkpoint, verification and publication had not yet been exercised; the subsequent lifecycle result is recorded below. Denial for a second authorized location and G5 remain open.

panel. Wasabi confirmed the `A testing draft` appears there, its details and

this read-only browser check made no changes. The page only requests detail
with the exact `read_detail` scope, derives
transition buttons from the matching location-scoped grants, requires an

are validated against the Gateway contracts. Gateway and database checks
remain authoritative; no grant or route policy changed. Cloudflare Worker
`ki-operator-control-plane` now serves source commit `32ef45ad` as version
`6ba59d2b-dc29-4b15-8caa-0ca00ac6b7e1`. `/ops`, `/field/records`, and

[#42747575851](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/42747575851)

Coolify deployment `ixqtocgchicjbabecwtbsi2m` finished on that commit; the
database, API, and worker are healthy. The role-bootstrap retry restored all
three bounded market-data function grants and passed relation/sequence
privilege checks, then rejected five PUBLIC-executable SECURITY DEFINER
trigger functions as if they were directly callable. PostgreSQL trigger
functions require trigger context and cannot be invoked as ordinary functions
([PostgreSQL trigger-function documentation](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-trigger.html)).
Because the provisioner uses autocommit, its exact bounded grants persisted;
readback confirms all three, with schema USAGE and no CREATE. No data was
imported, the worker feature flag remains false, and no recurring task ran.
The separate pre-import checkpoint `candidate-pre-world-bank-market-data-20261110T043635Z`
was captured after the Adelphi draft and bounded grants were present, committed
to `kokonut-backups`, and verified by readback ([evidence](builds/2026-11-10-recovery/candidate-market-data-bootstrap-retry.json)).
The follow-up excludes trigger-returning functions from the direct
callability audit, updates the source-contract assertion, and refreshes the
SQL inventory. Exact-head CI
[#42756752651](https://github.com/wasalo/Kokonut-Intelligence/actions/runs/42756752651)
passed preflight, source checks, and Kernel image; Security passed. Its
integration job failed only because the regression assertion searched the
grant-reconciliation block instead of the separate verification query. The
assertion scope was corrected. This historical blocker was closed by the
successful exact-head CI, candidate bootstrap, and one-shot import documented
immediately below.

### Candidate G5 current-weather one-shot — 9 November 2026

After the G4 read returned HTTP 200, the staged weather settings were confirmed present in both deployed Gateway and worker containers without exposing values. The signed-weather feature remained off in the long-running worker. The explicit `runtime-admin` role bootstrap provisioned `kokonut_worker_login` and reported no legacy compatibility grant; optional unrelated login passwords were blanked for this invocation. The bootstrap's checks enforce a nonprivileged, non-inheriting login with no direct relation grants and SET-only membership in `kokonut_worker_execution`.

A 30-minute `weather_observation:ingest` credential and grant were created for the exact active Adelphi location and tenant, then one OpenWeatherMap current-weather request inserted observation `e9f23df8-978b-43ed-859b-4c340ad8dac3`. The credential and scope grant were revoked and the temporary worker principal disabled immediately after the operation. The feature flag was enabled only in this ephemeral process. Forecast work, schedules, email, other providers, and other operational data writes were not run. See [environment presence evidence](builds/2026-11-09-recovery/g5-weather-worker-env-check.json) and [one-shot evidence](builds/2026-11-09-recovery/g5-weather-candidate-one-shot.json). This proves one candidate integration path only; it does not establish recurring operation, populated-data parity, or production acceptance.

### Operator session lifetime

The candidate's previous source defaulted to a 30-minute session lifetime,
which is too short for normal Control Plane work. Commit
`4be4d588b72e13d83f267a2a39ace3d80d4c9340` changes the Gateway and Compose
defaults to 12 hours, retaining the configured 5-minute minimum, 12-hour
maximum, server-side expiry, CSRF checks, and per-request capability
authorization. Exact-head CI and Security/Gitleaks passed, and Coolify
deployment `zwjtjcvqb3kzljmqiwjb1nur` finished on this commit. Public health
returns 200 and the unauthenticated setup endpoint returns 401 after rollout.
The previous browser session keeps its original expiry. A fresh sign-in starts
a new 12-hour session.

### 9 November pre-migration 752 checkpoint and restore

Before changing the candidate schema, both candidate PostgreSQL databases were
dumped directly into AES-256-CBC/PBKDF2 encryption, signed with the checkpoint
manifest, committed to `kokonut-backups/kokonut-kernel-backups`, and downloaded
back. Manifest, artifact hashes, and sizes matched. The encrypted Kokonut and
ZITADEL archives then restored successfully into separate, pinned PostgreSQL
16/PostGIS and PostgreSQL 17 containers on Docker's `none` network. The restore
used the repository's disabled NOLOGIN role prerequisites; it found 1,210
Kokonut user tables and 144 ZITADEL user tables, with PostGIS present. Only the
newly labeled temporary containers and volumes were removed after verification.
This is a fresh database checkpoint and isolated restore, not the full Coolify
recovery rehearsal or the 24-hour soak. See [checkpoint evidence](builds/2026-11-09-recovery/candidate-pre-migration-752-r2-checkpoint.json).

### Owner-selected industry-aligned alert severity and routing policy

Google SRE guidance favors timely, actionable alerts based on user-visible
symptoms. Prometheus Alertmanager supports grouping, routing, deduplication, and
inhibition to reduce repeated or cascading notifications ([Google SRE incident
management](https://sre.google/resources/practices-and-processes/incident-management-guide/),
[Prometheus Alertmanager concepts](https://prometheus.io/docs/alerting/latest/overview/),
[routing configuration](https://prometheus.io/docs/alerting/latest/configuration/)).
Wasabi selected industry-aligned routing for the candidate on 8 November 2026:

| Severity | Trigger | Candidate notification |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P1 — Critical | Broad user-facing outage, suspected compromise, confirmed data integrity loss, or imminent recovery-objective breach. | Once an acknowledged paging service is selected, page the on-call operator immediately and also send a record to `intelligence@kokonut.network`. Until then, email is best-effort and does not satisfy P1 paging acceptance. |
| P2 — High | Significant sustained capability degradation or repeated failure with user impact and a workaround. | Send an immediate, grouped email to `intelligence@kokonut.network`; on-call follow-up is expected. |
| P3 — Moderate | Localized degradation, one provider/job failing, or recoverable staleness without immediate broad impact. | Include in a daily digest to `intelligence@kokonut.network`; do not page. |
| P4 — Informational | Expected transitions and conditions without operator action. | Retain in logs/metrics; no email. |

Wasabi confirmed on 9 November 2026 that P1 page acceptance stays open until
an acknowledged paging service is selected. Email to
`intelligence@kokonut.network` is a notification record, not an acknowledged
page.

Group related alerts by service and incident, deduplicate repeats, and suppress
lower-level symptoms while a higher-severity cause is active. These are
owner-selected impact categories, not currently implemented routing rules. The
Gateway notification outbox currently uses one recipient and does not attach
these severities; candidate acceptance also requires a successful test delivery
and a decided P1 paging/acknowledgment path.

Cloudflare's official `cf` CLI is installed and OAuth-authenticated; a host-access
check on 8 November verified its default profile and R2/account-token scopes.
The repository sandbox cannot read the macOS Keychain that unlocks this profile,
so its `Not logged in` response is not evidence that OAuth approval failed.
The profile also has the Rules & Configuration permissions required for scoped
Configuration Rules. Wrangler is authenticated as well. A temporary user API
token restricted to DNS Write on the Kokonut zone was used for the `hub` DNS
cutover and has since been revoked. A hostname-specific Full (strict) rule is
active for `hub.kokonut.network`; the zone-wide SSL setting remains unchanged.
Two separate account-owned R2 S3 tokens were created
for `kokonut-kernel-media` and `kokonut-backups`, each restricted to its bucket
and the R2 object read/write/list permission group, with a 6 November 2027
expiry. Their S3 access-key pairs are encrypted in `.env.sops`; the one-time
API token values were not retained or printed. The R2 API creates S3 Access
Key IDs from token IDs and uses SHA-256 hashes of token values as Secret
Access Keys. Bucket policies cannot narrow access to an object prefix, so the media service's
prefix validation remains an important second boundary. Wrangler confirmed
the bucket inventory. From the VPS, read-only S3 `ListObjectsV2` requests for
`private/field-collector/` and `kokonut-kernel-backups/` initially returned HTTP 200 with
no matching objects. One synthetic media object was uploaded, read, deleted,
and confirmed absent. The later pre-migration candidate checkpoint is the only
backup object set written; its encrypted artifacts were read back and restored
into temporary network-isolated PostgreSQL containers and a separate internal
Compose recovery project. No production database or media content was accessed.
This validates candidate backup transport and database restore, not application
media integration, recurring backup cadence, or full Coolify application
recovery.
The report-export bucket and access policies were provisioned with the newly
verified host-access OAuth session. Its S3 credentials have not yet been used
for a candidate object request. See [R2 authentication](https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/api/tokens/)
and the [token creation API](https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/api/how-to/create-via-api/).

## Ordered implementation checklist

This is the active execution queue. Check a task only when its evidence exists;
preparation does not pass a gate by itself. G4 is the active deployment track;
source-only G2/G3 work proceeds alongside it without narrowing persistent grants.

November 2026. It is not an active G1–G7 task. Keep

after candidate sign-off.

  Standard plus bounded DuckDB/Parquet jobs and analytical workers, with
  optional Metabase; evaluate moving/reclassifying the current ClickHouse

  not need them.

  Caddy ingress; Analytics adds bounded DuckDB/Parquet analytical jobs and
  optional Metabase; Scale adds Redis, ClickHouse, extra worker capacity, and
  gRPC transport only when the accepted workload requires them. Preserve
  current smaller-tier behavior and avoid feature-specific forks in domain
  logic.
- [ ] Map every stateful component and scaling boundary. Recommended baseline
  to validate: Gateway replicas behind a health-aware ingress; one writable
  PostgreSQL primary with an explicitly selected failover/restore service;
  worker replicas governed by durable leases and idempotency; shared rate-limit
  behavior; R2-backed shared media/backups; and one migration coordinator.
  Define the exact Redis role (if any), ClickHouse rebuild boundary, and gRPC
  auth/network contract. Verify authentication/session and CSRF behavior across
  replicas. Do not assume container replication alone makes these safe.
- [ ] Set an explicit connection and resource budget across API replicas,
  workers, scheduler, migrations, and operator actions; define queue-lag and
  latency/error SLOs, monitoring, alert routing, and capacity headroom.
- [ ] Define and owner-approve Scale RPO/RTO, database and host failure
  behavior, restore source, rollout/rollback procedure, and maintenance window.
  Candidate 24-hour soak criteria do not supply production Scale targets.
- [ ] Prepare a future acceptance matrix for at least two API replicas and two
  worker replicas: auth/session/CSRF and shared rate-limit behavior;
  concurrent task claims with no duplicate business effects; lease
  expiry/recovery; bounded database
  connections; ingress and process failure; database failover and checkpoint
  restore; load/soak against the approved targets; and exact-digest upgrade and
  rollback.
- [ ] Review and approve this contract before a future implementation starts.
  A later release may enable `scale` only after the deployment overlay,
  planner/CI coverage, immutable multi-architecture artifacts, operational
  runbook, and all target-specific acceptance evidence exist. Scale remains a
  separate release milestone from Standard candidate sign-off.

## Completion register

Only this register changes active status. Status vocabulary: **open**, **in progress**, **blocked**, **passed**, **deferred**. A gate passes only when every applicable requirement has evidence; missing inputs remain blocked, not silently deferred. Responsible roles below are assignments of work, not claims that named people have accepted ownership.

| Gate / status | Responsible role; dependencies | Next action and acceptance criteria |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **G1 — Baseline/builds: in progress** | Implementer + reviewer; none | Capture source/schema/seed inventories and exact-head CI/Security. Verify both architecture images and package contracts; record artifact digests. Maintained signed Debian repositories may change transitive packages between builds: deployment reproducibility belongs to the tested image digest. Ordinary CI still rebuilds PostGIS; protected registry publication is separate. |
| **G2 — Database security: in progress** | Security implementer + caller owners; G1 | Owner selected separate API and general-worker logins. Verify the marked worker inherits only the existing runtime role, cannot `SET ROLE`, and does not receive the API credential; retain all existing grants while reviewing the full API/worker/CLI/ORM/dynamic SQL, Directus and external caller map. The optional Directus role/profile disposable checks now pass, but live external Directus deployments and permissions remain unverified. Scheduler and BMC source reviews identify remaining gaps; see the G2 caller-map checkpoint below and the [C1 cohort evidence](c1-runtime-acl-closure-plan.md). Narrow only by reviewed cohort using bounded functions/roles and tenant enforcement or approved global boundaries. Cover object-ID/location binding, wallet/account custody, credit-class administration, metric source lineage, append-only ledgers and same-transaction context. Prove fresh/repeated provisioning, table/column denials, two-tenant denial and supported-caller parity before any persistent role change. No blanket revocation based on source absence. |

Prepared integrations remain explicitly unaccepted until candidate evidence and owner disposition exist. |

Execute G1 first; progress G2 and G3 together where dependencies allow, collect external inputs immediately, then close G4/G5, G6 and finally G7. Run focused checks once per changed cohort; broaden only for changed integration surfaces, failures or unresolved concerns. Do not repeat costly builds for documentation-only updates.

## G2 implementation checkpoint — scheduler connection boundary

The scheduler loop now accepts the explicit `KOKONUT_SCHEDULER_DATABASE_URL` path through the dedicated worker connector, requiring login `kokonut_scheduler_login`. Selecting that variable (including an empty value), scheduler isolation, or the shared-database deny marker prevents fallback to the runtime connector. Legacy unselected operation remains compatible. Ninety-three focused scheduler/connection/durability/supervisor tests and Ruff passed. No deployment flag or role was provisioned; G2 remains open.

Before enabling this path, implement migration-backed scheduler grants, bootstrap/Compose routing, schema-readiness and worker-health reads, and fresh/upgrade disposable acceptance. The scheduler owns claims, runs and resource slots, but completion also directly inserts `platform_event`; migrate that event intent behind a bounded contract rather than restoring shared-runtime event DML. Keep definition administration distinct from execution updates. Preserve API child PG settings and exact isolated job-secret filtering. This is source preparation, not API/general-worker separation acceptance or candidate rollout.

## G2 implementation checkpoint — bounded scheduler events (migration 747)

Source now replaces the scheduler's direct `platform_event` INSERT with `kokonut_security.enqueue_scheduler_run_event(run_id, worker_id)`. It derives event type/payload from the persisted terminal run, checks worker binding, and records an event receipt atomically for retry-safe enqueue. A non-login owner has only the required source columns and event insert columns; PUBLIC execution is revoked. The dedicated scheduler role receives function execution but no direct event or receipt access. Runtime provisioning reapplies the compatibility caller's execute grant when the function exists.

The network-disabled disposable PostgreSQL checker applies/reapplies migration 747 and verifies duplicate enqueue, denied wrong-worker/nonterminal access, denied direct event/receipt access, and transaction rollback. The scheduler/durability/runtime-role selection passed 146 tests; Ruff and source migration validation pass. This is focused synthetic acceptance, not full-schema/RLS acceptance, scheduler-login provisioning, or candidate rollout. Source now contains 738 migrations; the inspected candidate remains through 745. Before cutover, finish execution-column grants, bootstrap/Compose credentials, readiness/health access, full-schema fresh/upgrade checks, and API/general-worker privilege separation. G2 stays open.

Run `python3 scripts/check-scheduler-run-event-disposable.py` for this focused fixture; it starts only a temporary network-disabled PostgreSQL container, uses no persistent target, and removes the container afterward.

## G2 implementation checkpoint — scheduler executor provisioning (migration 748)

Migration 748 gives the dedicated scheduler role read access to definitions/run state and column-specific execution writes, not definition administration, run deletion, resource-limit changes or direct event writes. Queue health can read status/timing columns only; readiness can read migration IDs/checksums/status. The optional runtime bootstrap provisions `kokonut_scheduler_login` only when `KOKONUT_SCHEDULER_PASSWORD` is supplied. Its transaction handles both normal and existing-bootstrap autocommit connections, rejects weak/reused primary credentials, unexpected execution-column grants, foreign mutations, ownership/privileged attributes and other-database CONNECT access. No caller receives broad cloned runtime grants.

The explicit `docker-compose.kernel-scheduler-role.yml` overlay is appended **after** Standard/runtime-role and selected architecture/service manifests by `standard --scheduler-role`. It sets only worker PG credentials and the required `KOKONUT_SCHEDULER_DATABASE_URL`; API settings remain unchanged. The planner requires both scheduler variables in the environment, keeps their values out of printed commands, and accepts the overlay in `release-up` because it adds no image. Configure the matching scheduler DSN/password through SOPS; never copy the API password. Provision after migrations 747/748, before enabling the overlay. Standard does not select this overlay by default. The scheduler child strips shared PG credentials and uses its dedicated connector; the raw bootstrap password is excluded from all child environments.

The focused network-disabled PostgreSQL checker runs actual scheduler claim/resource/completion/event code and queue-health/readiness reads under the restricted role. Provision/reprovision and stale broad-grant rejection pass; 91 focused regressions and Ruff pass. The full Standard disposable acceptance now also composes the scheduler-role overlay, applies the complete 739-migration source snapshot through 748, provisions and re-provisions the dedicated login, verifies the API/worker identities, and exercises worker health, stalled-child detection, database outage/recovery, and clean shutdown. It passed with no failed migrations and no candidate, legacy, or production changes ([evidence](builds/2026-11-05-recovery/standard-scheduler-role-748-disposable.json)). This is synthetic candidate-profile acceptance, not RLS/two-tenant, populated-data, hardware, candidate rollout, or production acceptance. The inspected persistent candidate remains unchanged through 745.

The scheduler provisioner now also audits effective `SECURITY DEFINER` execution across user schemas and rejects every callable function except the bounded scheduler-event function; trigger-returning routines are not SQL-callable. The candidate's read-only catalog audit found its five PUBLIC-executable `SECURITY DEFINER` routines are all trigger functions. The focused disposable acceptance verifies a PUBLIC-callable synthetic function is rejected, then provisioning succeeds once it is revoked. The full check is `KOKONUT_STANDARD_RECOVERY_TIMEOUT=900 python3 scripts/check-kernel-standard-recovery-disposable.py`; it uses synthetic credentials, a portless internal network, and a unique Compose project that is removed on exit. The focused checker remains `python3 scripts/check-scheduler-executor-disposable.py`.

The scheduler writer review found `--enable` and `--disable` were the only CLI definition mutations, with no Gateway route and no creator/owner attribution on `scheduled_task`. Those CLI mutations fail before database access in isolated scheduler mode. The one-shot `--tick` CLI now shares the scheduler connector and uses the dedicated login whenever the DSN/isolation marker is selected. `--run-now` directly executes a module without claims, run history or completion events, so it now fails before database access in isolated scheduler mode; the old compatibility behavior remains for unselected legacy environments. The focused scheduler/CLI/contract suite passes 172 tests, including fail-closed checks for each isolation selector and definition mutation. The profile planner opt-in/release contract and ARM64 overlay ordering pass all 112 `tests/test_kernel_profile.py` checks.

## G2 implementation checkpoint — authenticated scheduler definition operations (migration 749)

The Gateway now exposes a bounded schedule inventory plus enable/disable routes under OIDC global capabilities `scheduler:read`, `scheduler:enable`, and `scheduler:disable`. Mutations require CSRF and a non-empty reason. Enable uses the existing Python dependency-graph validation; disable rejects active retries, running work, and enabled transitive dependents. Each changed task is recorded in `kokonut_security.scheduler_operator_audit` by a session-bound security-definer function in the same transaction as the state change. The isolated scheduler executor still has no definition-administration grants; the legacy mutation CLI still fails closed when isolated.

Migration 749 and route authorization pass a network-disabled PostgreSQL acceptance and 90 focused Gateway-auth/capability tests; Ruff, compilation, schema validation, and dependency-free preflight pass. The disposable acceptance verifies the OIDC-role boundary, scheduler executor denial, action audit attribution, and no direct audit-table access ([evidence](builds/2026-11-05-recovery/scheduler-operator-749-disposable.json)). CI #1099 passed on parent SHA `811a9e43`; exact-head Security, source, and multi-architecture image checks pass on `ede48b37`, while integration remains in progress. This slice does not close API/general-worker identity separation, provide schedule creation/owner attribution, or establish candidate acceptance. Migration 749 is not applied to the persistent candidate. CI now runs `python3 scripts/check-scheduler-operator-disposable.py` in its disposable authorization phase.

The G2 schedule review found that historical seed 050 is not the candidate's persisted schedule state. It contains legacy entries that are no-ops, have missing inputs, require per-chain/per-location expansion, or lack a dedicated isolated writer. The candidate currently has zero persisted scheduler rows, so no schedule or seed was changed. Future registration still needs approved chain/location/driver inputs and bounded writer identities; keep `--scheduler-role` opt-in. Source commit `755e531d` closes one fail-open edge: a scheduled child with no operation identity retains shared-database stripping and the deny marker whenever its parent carries the deny marker, scheduler isolation marker, or dedicated scheduler DSN. Commit `26cd96fe` aligns scheduler isolation detection with connection selection when the scheduler DSN variable is present, including an empty value. The child receives no worker identity or worker DSN. The focused worker-environment suite passes 45 tests and Ruff passes. The route-policy inventory now includes concrete UUID examples for scheduler read/enable/disable endpoints; all 8 tests in that inventory file and Ruff pass. The generated worker function graph is refreshed for the changed environment-helper line references; its check and 13 focused tests pass. These guards and inventory entries do not establish task-by-task schedule readiness or candidate acceptance.

## G2 implementation checkpoint — operator scope reads (migration 750)

The Gateway OIDC provider now uses the dedicated `kokonut_operator_auth` connection for `has_scope` and `list_scopes`. Migration 750 adds two `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` read functions owned by `kokonut_operator_scope_read_owner`, a non-login role with only the tenant-predicate columns it needs. The auth login can execute those functions and cannot directly read scope, identity, organization, or location tables; the general runtime login cannot execute the new functions. Existing tenant membership, verified-owner, active-share, share-expiry, disabled-identity, legacy location-only grant, and response-order behavior are preserved. Runtime-role provisioning reasserts the function grants, verifies ownership, and denies PUBLIC execution; the DB-only restore prerequisites include the new owner role.

Focused OIDC/restore contracts and Ruff pass. The network-disabled disposable PostgreSQL 16 acceptance applies and replays migration 750, forces RLS on the seven scope relations, and confirms caller behavior, direct-read denials, and function ACLs ([evidence](builds/2026-11-06-recovery/operator-scope-read-750-disposable.json)). It does not narrow the pre-existing general runtime table grants, establish two-tenant parity for all Gateway SQL, or close G2. The acceptance entrypoint is `python3 scripts/check-operator-scope-read-disposable.py`.

## Owner inputs and deferred milestones

Existing decisions stand: Gateway-native farm activity, optional Directus, self-hosted ZITADEL, all repository-backed reports first, Kokonut Network's five named locations, Maileroo SMTP, R2 production/backups buckets, USA/Dominican Republic dynamic retention, and 24-hour recovery target. Do not re-ask settled choices. Keep unconfirmed tenant sharing ungranted.

Owner inputs received on 5 November 2026: the G2 source-known caller map under B2 was confirmed by the repository owner; this records the map only and does not prove effective Directus permissions or resolve unknown external callers before any grant change. **Wasabi, Secretary of Kokonut, is the single accountable owner for MAN-CAP outcomes across farm/Collector, governance/agents, finance/credits, reports/providers, and administration.** The outcomes remain open until evidence and sign-off are recorded. The candidate runs on the Coolify VPS at `167.88.45.47` (x86_64), Coolify project `qwo0g0o40c48s4ccwcowwcw0`, environment `b84s4c8k8w48kscows4w8cw4`. Cloudflare Workers Static Assets for the three browser apps and private R2 buckets for media, report exports, and backups are the approved direction. At the time of this 5 November input, ZITADEL and Cloudflare deployment were still being configured; the completed candidate cutover is recorded in the current status above. Wasabi authorized replacing the former Baserow `hub` record and retiring Baserow, with no alternate Baserow hostname.

Follow-up G4–G6 inputs from Wasabi: the Cloudflare apex zone is `kokonut.network`. The candidate ZITADEL issuer and confidential client have since been configured and deployed as described above. Wasabi authorized replacing the former `hub` Baserow record and retiring Baserow, with no alternate Baserow hostname. No populated Adelphi/report reference data is available yet. Alert recipient `intelligence@kokonut.network` is configured in SOPS, and Wasabi owns the on-call rota. Candidate SMTP TLS/authentication and NOOP passed; one static message was delivered and inbox receipt confirmed. Wasabi selected the severity policy above; event-driven routing and P1 paging/acknowledgment remain open. Wasabi chose to use proposed G6 defaults. The working candidate-only defaults are: a 24-hour VPS soak with `/health` sampled every minute and at least 99.5% successful probes outside the planned drill; RPO no greater than 24 hours using a verified age-encrypted PostgreSQL checkpoint staged to the private `kokonut-backups` R2 bucket; recovery from a database service restart within 15 minutes; and an isolated checkpoint restore verified healthy within two hours. Wasabi will serve as the independent G6 reviewer and sign-off owner. The implementation agent (Codex) will operate the drill after G4/G5 prerequisites are met, keeping operator and reviewer separate. The controlled drill is one planned PostgreSQL stop/restart followed by restore to a new isolated recovery project, never over the serving database. Bucket-scoped R2 credentials and a fresh `BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` are now configured in SOPS. Backup cadence, transfer verification, isolated restore evidence, and the approved recovery window remain missing, so these defaults do not pass G6 and do not set production targets.

Inputs still needed: independent/live confirmation of effective Directus and external caller access before narrowing grants; trusted-browser test-user/role and callback/session review; an approved populated farm/report source or decision on how to obtain equivalent owner-approved reference data; report disclosure review; candidate provider activation status and approved cadence overrides (Wasabi owns operations; code/SOPS variable names and retry defaults are now mapped); implementation and acceptance of event-driven severity routing, and a P1 paging/acknowledgment path; backup cadence, a scheduled recovery window, verified bucket-scoped R2 transfer and isolated restore, and a published runbook applying the candidate-only RPO/RTO defaults with measured evidence. Secrets go through SOPS, never the guide or chat. Reconcile historical access requests against current authorization before asking again.

**Deferred beyond candidate completion:** public DNS/TLS and production topology/RPO acceptance, production failover/scale, physical Pi thermal/storage/power-loss testing, production retention activation, release promotion and legacy retirement. Candidate scope/tenant, populated parity, backup restoration and required human review cannot be deferred by an implementer. Production pruning remains disabled until policy owners approve periods and holds; the candidate must prove no-prune and hold behavior. No hardware acceptance is claimed without hardware.

## Evidence and maintenance protocol

For each acceptance result record gate/capability, source SHA and dirty state, schema snapshot, image digest, target/project, command, time, outcome, limitations and cleanup/rollback. Distinguish source tests, disposable acceptance, candidate acceptance and owner decisions. Preserve dated artifacts unchanged and supersede by link.

Update this guide's register and capability acceptance rows after each batch. Capability-matrix classifications remain supporting catalog metadata; update them only when their existing criteria change and run its validator. Append history to progress; do not synchronize competing active TODOs or checklist totals. A newly pushed SHA reopens exact-head CI acceptance until checks pass.

Validation commands: `python3 scripts/check-capability-matrix.py`, `python3 -m services.migration validate --schemas-only`, `python3 scripts/check-scheduler-operator-disposable.py`, plus cohort-specific checks in contributor instructions and specialist runbooks. Use [Standard procedures](standard-profile.md), [candidate procedures](kernel-candidate.md), [C1 technical detail](c1-runtime-acl-closure-plan.md), [recovery preparation](recovery-preparation.md) and [provider inputs](open-follow-ups.md) as references; they cannot override this register.

## Requirement reconciliation index

The entries below account for every unchecked checkbox found in the pre-consolidation Markdown corpus. They are **source references, not another task queue**. Repeated historical CI/replay items roll into current-head G1/G6. Historical implementation items remain under their gate until source/evidence review proves supersession; their old checkbox is not authority to repeat work. Mixed requirements map to multiple gates. Detailed prose, tables, decisions and contracts remain supporting specifications under G2–G6, including requirements without checkboxes.

Seven focused mocked checks pass without database/provider access ([batch evidence](builds/2026-10-29-recovery/kernel-next-contract-batch.json)). The Prefect-compatible `JobFailed` wrapper now excludes raw child stderr from exception text, retaining only the label/exit code while keeping the historical bounded output attribute. Its focused 41-test JobSpec contract module passes; no live provider or scheduler was exercised ([evidence](builds/2026-10-30-recovery/provider-job-error-and-catalog-parity.json)). The climate fan-out now also records only the safe `JobFailed` message in its aggregate result rather than propagating bounded raw stderr into Prefect task state. Its mock-only location/exit-code regression passes; provider and database access remain untested and untouched ([evidence](builds/2026-10-30-recovery/climate-fanout-stderr-redaction.json)). The retained legacy health-check and Yahoo price-feed flows also omit child stderr from raised exception text; two mocked regressions pass. This hardens error reporting only: it does not register/activate those flows or close the Yahoo persistence and attestation contract ([evidence](builds/2026-10-30-recovery/legacy-prefect-error-redaction.json); [Yahoo audit](builds/2026-10-28-recovery/yahoo-price-feed-contract-audit.json)). A further source audit found composite analytics and six-hour Prefect flows still dispatch the legacy stored-SQL dashboard job when its feature is off by default. The composite paths now skip that stage with an explicit reason and continue credit/remote-sensing work; standalone refresh remains fail-closed. The same audit found the daily/integration composites invoked the default-off isolated market-data worker unconditionally; Prefect dispatch now reports an explicit skip until enabled, with direct worker access still fail-closed. Focused workflow/worker tests pass 30 checks, and Prefect runtime acceptance passes five more through the approved host path. These slices remain open pending CI and completion of analytics acceptance. Sources: [provider/job inventory](batches.md#L2288), [worker recovery](batches.md#L2358).
- **R091 → G5:** [todo-kernel-refactor.md, original line 1742](todo-kernel-refactor.md) — **C5 — **C5 — Close source-verifiable analytics and reporting gaps.** Port registered queries/cards and deterministic projections, fix correctness and access gaps, and preserve ClickHouse as rebuildable optional projection. Direct report generators now reject missing location scope before database access, consistent with the registry and CLI; reviewed global rows remain visible only under a selected location, and unlineaged network rows are omitted from farm-scoped reports. The integrated report suite passes 563 tests with four database-dependent skips. Its generated inventory records 94 report types, 167 direct query sites, 166 positional-bind sites, and zero unresolved sites or bind mismatches. `climate_impact`, `revenue_multiplier`, `environmental`, and `ecological_modeling` also reject missing location before opening a cursor or starting the analyzer; focused selections pass 2, 125, 2, and 2 tests respectively. The EBF scorecard now rejects a missing/empty location before cursor creation; the focused source selection passes 324 checks. The inventory's 13 entries with no visible location-bound SQL signal were reviewed: twelve are deliberate network reports with explicit network-scope guards, and `business_plan` rejects missing scope while its all-location mode is intentional. Do not infer populated-farm parity from synthetic fixtures. Tactical-layer companion lookups now keep target threats within the selected location, and the State-of-Kokonut actor/funding sections filter all four queries to selected locations while rejecting empty selections; network-wide mode is unchanged. Their focused suites pass 73 and 285 tests respectively, with report inventory freshness checks ([tactical scope](builds/2026-10-28-recovery/tactical-layer-companion-location-scope.json); [State of Kokonut scope](builds/2026-10-28-recovery/state-of-kokonut-selected-actor-scope.json)). The latest source audit found remaining location-unfiltered report cases are explicit network/public projections, an explicit all-location operation, or stakeholder/global surfaces whose audience and scope cannot be safely changed without populated fixtures and owner review; no additional query change was selected. Ecological residual averages now exclude rows without measurements and weight only measured input mass in both report and analytics adapters. The focused report/analytics suite passes 56 tests, including mixed and all-unmeasured cases ([batch evidence](builds/2026-10-29-recovery/kernel-next-contract-batch.json)). A subsequent correctness audit found the weather-growth query summed harvest and rainfall after joining each harvest to every matching 30-day weather observation. The query now aggregates verified harvest totals and distinct matched weather observations separately, preserves the existing result grain and response shape, and excludes harvest/cycle location mismatches. A second audit found revenue and expense rows were joined directly at crop-cycle grain before both sums; the analytics query now aggregates each event family per eligible cycle first and rejects event/cycle location mismatches. Disposable PostgreSQL 16 acceptance now passes with synthetic rows: overlapping weather windows return 15 kg harvest, two events, three unique observations, 10 mm rainfall, and exclude mismatched event/cycle locations; revenue totals $300 against $60 expense (400% ROI), with no-event cycles at zero and mismatched locations excluded. The analytics source slice is verified, while C5 remains open for broader parity and reporting work ([evidence](builds/2026-10-29-recovery/analytics-event-aggregation-disposable.json)). Completed source slices: stakeholder trust/identity readers now use exact migration-backed projections; pest-management and marketplace CLI date/JSON inputs fail before PostgreSQL access; the migration-517 configuration own- drafts queue now has a strict, location/page-bound Gateway and console contract with bounded continuation. These improve source contracts but do not close the broader populated-data reporting parity gate. - [x] Pin migration-222/227 stakeholder trust and identity-resolution read projections while preserving their current filters, order, payload, and access boundary. - [x] Preflight all pest-management CLI dates and marketplace harvest dates and buyer JSON objects before database acquisition. - [x] Validate the private migration-517 configuration own-drafts response and operator-console page, including ordering and the 10,000-offset cap. | CAP-060 | Reports / providers | Schema.org alignment | G2–G6; sign-off G7 | open; consult [catalog](capability-matrix.md) for contract/manual evidence |
| CAP-061 | Finance / credits | Regen Network parity | G2–G6; sign-off G7 | open; consult [catalog](capability-matrix.md) for contract/manual evidence |
| CAP-062 | Finance / credits | ARKIV parity | G2–G6; sign-off G7 | open; consult [catalog](capability-matrix.md) for contract/manual evidence |
| CAP-063 | Reports / providers | GeoNode parity | G2–G6; sign-off G7 | open; consult [catalog](capability-matrix.md) for contract/manual evidence |
| CAP-064 | Administration | gRPC API | G2–G6; sign-off G7 | open; consult [catalog](capability-matrix.md) for contract/manual evidence |
| CAP-065 | Reports / providers | Business Model Canvas | G2–G6; sign-off G7 | open; consult [catalog](capability-matrix.md) for contract/manual evidence |
| CAP-066 | Reports / providers | State of Kokonut | G2–G6; sign-off G7 | open; consult [catalog](capability-matrix.md) for contract/manual evidence |
| CAP-067 | Governance / agents | Kokonut Protocol execution bridge | G2–G6; sign-off G7 | open; consult [catalog](capability-matrix.md) for contract/manual evidence |

The primary group is the first review route; Wasabi remains accountable for all five groups. The assignments cover 10 Farm/Collector, 20 Governance/agents, 14 Finance/credits, 16 Reports/providers, and 7 Administration rows. Wasabi confirmed source claims for all 13 cross-cutting CAPs; candidate acceptance stays open until its evidence and outcomes are recorded.
